<p>The <b>Cocos (Keeling) Islands</b> (<a href="/wiki/Cocos_Islands_Malay_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Cocos Islands Malay language">Cocos Islands Malay</a>: <i lang="coa">Pulu Kokos [Keeling]</i>), officially the <b>Territory of Cocos (Keeling) Islands</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;k&#39; in &#39;kind&#39;">k</span><span title="/oʊ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;code&#39;">oʊ</span><span title="&#39;k&#39; in &#39;kind&#39;">k</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span><span title="&#39;s&#39; in &#39;sigh&#39;">s</span></span>/</a></span></span>;<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Cocos_Islands_Malay_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Cocos Islands Malay language">Cocos Islands Malay</a>: <i lang="coa">Pulu Kokos [Keeling]</i>), are an <a href="/wiki/States_and_territories_of_Australia" title="States and territories of Australia">Australian external territory</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Indian_Ocean" title="Indian Ocean">Indian Ocean</a>, comprising a small archipelago approximately midway between <a href="/wiki/Australia" title="Australia">Australia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka" title="Sri Lanka">Sri Lanka</a> and relatively close to the Indonesian island of <a href="/wiki/Sumatra" title="Sumatra">Sumatra</a>. The territory's <a href="/wiki/Dual_naming" title="Dual naming">dual name</a> (official since the islands' incorporation into Australia in 1955) reflects that the islands have historically been known as either the <b>Cocos Islands</b> or the <b>Keeling Islands</b>.
</p><p>The territory consists of two <a href="/wiki/Atoll" title="Atoll">atolls</a> made up of 27 <a href="/wiki/Coral_island" title="Coral island">coral islands</a>, of which only two – <a href="/wiki/West_Island,_Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands" title="West Island, Cocos (Keeling) Islands">West Island</a> and <a href="/wiki/Home_Island" title="Home Island">Home Island</a> – are inhabited. The population of around 600 people consists mainly of <a href="/wiki/Cocos_Malays" title="Cocos Malays">Cocos Malays</a>, who mostly practice <a href="/wiki/Sunni_Islam" title="Sunni Islam">Sunni Islam</a> and speak <a href="/wiki/Cocos_Malay" title="Cocos Malay">a dialect of Malay</a> as their first language.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> The territory is administered by the Australian federal government's <a href="/wiki/Department_of_Infrastructure,_Transport,_Regional_Development_and_Communications" title="Department of Infrastructure, Transport, Regional Development and Communications">Department of Infrastructure, Transport, Regional Development and Communications</a> as an Australian external territory and together with <a href="/wiki/Christmas_Island" title="Christmas Island">Christmas Island</a> (which is about 960 kilometres (600&#160;mi) to the east) forms the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Indian_Ocean_Territories" title="Australian Indian Ocean Territories">Australian Indian Ocean Territories</a> administrative grouping. However, the islanders do have a degree of self-government through <a href="/wiki/Shire_of_Cocos" class="mw-redirect" title="Shire of Cocos">the local shire council</a>. Many public services – including health, education, and policing – are provided by the state of <a href="/wiki/Western_Australia" title="Western Australia">Western Australia</a>, and Western Australian law applies except where the federal government has determined otherwise. The territory also uses Western Australian postcodes.
</p><p>The islands were discovered in 1609 by the British sea captain <a href="/wiki/William_Keeling" title="William Keeling">William Keeling</a>, but no settlement occurred until the early 19th century. One of the first settlers was John Clunies-Ross, a Scottish merchant; much of the island's current population is descended from the Malay workers he brought in to work his <a href="/wiki/Copra" title="Copra">copra</a> plantation. The <a href="/wiki/Clunies-Ross_family" title="Clunies-Ross family">Clunies-Ross family</a> ruled the islands as a private fiefdom for almost 150 years, with the head of the family usually recognised as <a href="/wiki/Resident_magistrate" title="Resident magistrate">resident magistrate</a>. The British annexed the islands in 1857, and for the next century they were administered from either <a href="/wiki/British_Ceylon" title="British Ceylon">Ceylon</a> or <a href="/wiki/Colony_of_Singapore" title="Colony of Singapore">Singapore</a>. The territory was transferred to Australia in 1955, although until 1979 virtually all of the territory's <a href="/wiki/Real_estate" title="Real estate">real estate</a> still belonged to the Clunies-Ross family.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Name">Name</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Name">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Lagoon_Beach_(25152057091).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Lagoon_Beach_%2825152057091%29.jpg/300px-Lagoon_Beach_%2825152057091%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="300" height="225" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Lagoon_Beach_%2825152057091%29.jpg/450px-Lagoon_Beach_%2825152057091%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Lagoon_Beach_%2825152057091%29.jpg/600px-Lagoon_Beach_%2825152057091%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a><figcaption>Home Island Beach</figcaption></figure>
<p>The islands have been called the <i>Cocos Islands</i> (from 1622), the <i>Keeling Islands</i> (from 1703), the <i>Cocos–Keeling Islands</i> (since <a href="/wiki/James_Horsburgh" title="James Horsburgh">James Horsburgh</a> in 1805) and the <i>Keeling–Cocos Islands</i> (19th century).<sup id="cite_ref-arb399p1t2_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-arb399p1t2-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> <i>Cocos</i> refers to the abundant <a href="/wiki/Coconut" title="Coconut">coconut</a> trees, while <i>Keeling</i> refers to <a href="/wiki/William_Keeling" title="William Keeling">William Keeling</a>, who discovered the islands in 1609.<sup id="cite_ref-arb399p1t2_9-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-arb399p1t2-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>John Clunies-Ross,<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> who sailed there in the <i>Borneo</i> in 1825, called the group the <i>Borneo Coral Isles</i>, restricting <i>Keeling</i> to <a href="/wiki/North_Keeling" title="North Keeling">North Keeling</a>, and calling South Keeling "the Cocos properly so called".<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> The form <i>Cocos (Keeling) Islands</i>, attested from 1916,<sup id="cite_ref-WeberWeber1916_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WeberWeber1916-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> was made official by the Cocos (Keeling) Islands Act 1955.<sup id="cite_ref-arb399p1t2_9-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-arb399p1t2-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The territory's Malay name is <i>Pulu Kokos (Keeling)</i>. Sign boards on the island also feature Malay translations.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The Cocos (Keeling) Islands consist of two flat, low-lying coral atolls with an area of 14.2 square kilometres (5.5&#160;sq&#160;mi), 26 kilometres (16&#160;mi) of coastline, a highest elevation of 5 metres (16&#160;ft) and thickly covered with coconut palms and other vegetation. The climate is pleasant, moderated by the southeast <a href="/wiki/Trade_winds" title="Trade winds">trade winds</a> for about nine months of the year and with moderate rainfall. <a href="/wiki/Tropical_cyclone" title="Tropical cyclone">Tropical cyclones</a> may occur in the early months of the year.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/North_Keeling" title="North Keeling">North Keeling</a> Island is an atoll consisting of just one C-shaped island, a nearly closed atoll ring with a small opening into the lagoon, about 50 metres (160&#160;ft) wide, on the east side. The island measures 1.1 square kilometres (270 acres) in land area and is uninhabited. The lagoon is about 0.5 square kilometres (120 acres). North Keeling Island and the surrounding sea to 1.5&#160;km (0.93&#160;mi) from shore form the <a href="/wiki/Pulu_Keeling_National_Park" class="mw-redirect" title="Pulu Keeling National Park">Pulu Keeling National Park</a>, established on 12 December 1995. It is home to the only surviving population of the endemic, and endangered, <a href="/wiki/Cocos_Buff-banded_Rail" class="mw-redirect" title="Cocos Buff-banded Rail">Cocos Buff-banded Rail</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/South_Keeling_Islands" title="South Keeling Islands">South Keeling Islands</a> is an atoll consisting of 24 individual islets forming an incomplete atoll ring, with a total land area of 13.1 square kilometres (5.1&#160;sq&#160;mi). Only <a href="/wiki/Home_Island" title="Home Island">Home Island</a> and <a href="/wiki/West_Island,_Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands" title="West Island, Cocos (Keeling) Islands">West Island</a> are populated. The Cocos Malays maintain weekend shacks, referred to as pondoks, on most of the larger islands.
</p>

<p>There are no rivers or lakes on either atoll. Fresh water resources are limited to <a href="/wiki/Lens_(hydrology)" title="Lens (hydrology)">water lenses</a> on the larger islands, underground accumulations of rainwater lying above the seawater. These lenses are accessed through shallow bores or wells.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Flora_and_fauna">Flora and fauna</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Flora and fauna">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Flora_of_the_Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands" title="Flora of the Cocos (Keeling) Islands">Flora of the Cocos (Keeling) Islands</a> and <a href="/wiki/Fauna_of_the_Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands" title="Fauna of the Cocos (Keeling) Islands">Fauna of the Cocos (Keeling) Islands</a></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Cocos (Keeling) Islands experience a <a href="/wiki/Tropical_rainforest_climate" title="Tropical rainforest climate">tropical rainforest climate</a> (<i>Af</i>) according to the <a href="/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification" title="Köppen climate classification">Köppen climate classification</a>; the archipelago lies approximately midway between the <a href="/wiki/Equator" title="Equator">equator</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Tropic_of_Capricorn" title="Tropic of Capricorn">Tropic of Capricorn</a>. The archipelago has two distinct seasons, the wet season and the dry season. The wettest month is April with precipitation totaling 262.6 millimetres (10.34&#160;in), and the driest month is October with precipitation totaling 88.2 millimetres (3.47&#160;in). Due to the strong maritime control, temperatures vary little although its location is some distance from the <a href="/wiki/Equator" title="Equator">Equator</a>. The hottest month is March with an average high temperature of 30.0&#160;°C (86.0&#160;°F), while the coolest month is September with an average low temperature of 24.2&#160;°C (75.6&#160;°F).
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>According to the 2021 Australian Census, the current population of the Cocos Islands is 593 people.<sup id="cite_ref-census_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> The median age of the population is 40 years, slightly older than the median Australian population age of 38 years.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> As of 2021, there are no people living on the Cocos Islands who identify as Indigenous Australians (Aboriginal or Torres Strait Islander).<sup id="cite_ref-census_3-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="PieChartTemplate thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px">
<div class="mw-no-invert" style="background-color:white;margin:auto;position:relative;width:200px;height:200px;overflow:hidden;border-radius:100px;border:1px solid black;transform:scaleX(-1)rotate(-90deg)">
<div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:0; top:0; border-width:0 200px 200px 0; border-color:Red"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 991.36510217697px; border-left-color:Grey"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:Grey"></div>

<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:Turquoise"></div>

<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:Yellow"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:Yellow"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;right:100px; top:100px; border-width:83.059589919581px 55.687561648819px 0 0; border-top-color:Green"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:Green"></div>
</div>
<div class="thumbcaption">
<p>Religion in Cocos Islands (2021) <sup id="cite_ref-census_3-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r981673959">.mw-parser-output .legend{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .legend-color{display:inline-block;min-width:1.25em;height:1.25em;line-height:1.25;margin:1px 0;text-align:center;border:1px solid black;background-color:transparent;color:black}.mw-parser-output .legend-text{}</style><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Green; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islam</a> (65.6%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Yellow; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Non-religious" class="mw-redirect" title="Non-religious">Non-religious</a> (14%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Blue; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholic">Catholic</a> (2%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Turquoise; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Anglican (1.5%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Grey; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Unspecified (15.3%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Red; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Other (1.6%)</div>
</div>
</div></div>
<p>The majority religion of the Cocos Islands is Islam, with 65.6% of the total population identifying as Muslim, followed by Unspecified (15.3%), Non-religious (14.0%), Catholic (2.0%), Anglican (1.5%). The remaining 1.6% of Cocos Islanders identify as secular or hold various other beliefs (including atheism, agnosticism and unspecified spiritual beliefs).<sup id="cite_ref-census_3-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li class="mw-empty-elt"></li></ul>
<p>73.5% of the population were born in Australia - either on the mainland, on the Cocos Islands, or in another Australian territory. The remaining 26.5% born outside of Australia come from various countries, including Malaysia (4.0%), England (1.3%), New Zealand (1.2%), Singapore (0.5%) and Argentina (0.5%), among others.<sup id="cite_ref-census_3-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> 61.2% of the population speak Malay rather than English at home, while 19.1% use English as their primary language and 3.5% speak another language (including Spanish and various Austronesian and African languages).<sup id="cite_ref-census_3-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Kaum_Ibu_(Women%27s_Group)" title="Kaum Ibu (Women&#39;s Group)">Kaum Ibu (Women's Group)</a> is a <a href="/wiki/Women%27s_rights" title="Women&#39;s rights">women's rights</a> organisation that represents the view of women at a local and national level.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Discovery_and_early_history">Discovery and early history</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Discovery and early history">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:AMH-5134-NA_Compass_chart_of_the_Kokos_islands.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/27/AMH-5134-NA_Compass_chart_of_the_Kokos_islands.jpg/220px-AMH-5134-NA_Compass_chart_of_the_Kokos_islands.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="159" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/27/AMH-5134-NA_Compass_chart_of_the_Kokos_islands.jpg/330px-AMH-5134-NA_Compass_chart_of_the_Kokos_islands.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/27/AMH-5134-NA_Compass_chart_of_the_Kokos_islands.jpg/440px-AMH-5134-NA_Compass_chart_of_the_Kokos_islands.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2400" data-file-height="1734" /></a><figcaption>Historic compass chart of the Cocos islands<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<p>The archipelago was discovered in 1609 by Captain <a href="/wiki/William_Keeling" title="William Keeling">William Keeling</a> of the <a href="/wiki/East_India_Company" title="East India Company">East India Company</a>, on a return voyage from the East Indies. <a href="/wiki/North_Keeling" title="North Keeling">North Keeling</a> was sketched by Ekeberg, a Swedish captain, in 1749, showing the presence of <a href="/wiki/Coconut" title="Coconut">coconut palms</a>. It also appears on a 1789 chart produced by British hydrographer <a href="/wiki/Alexander_Dalrymple" title="Alexander Dalrymple">Alexander Dalrymple</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-PKNPMP_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PKNPMP-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1825, Scottish merchant seaman Captain <a href="/wiki/John_Clunies-Ross" class="mw-redirect" title="John Clunies-Ross">John Clunies-Ross</a> stopped briefly at the islands on a trip to India, nailing up a <a href="/wiki/Union_Jack" title="Union Jack">Union Jack</a> and planning to return and settle on the islands with his family in the future.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> Wealthy Englishman <a href="/wiki/Alexander_Hare" title="Alexander Hare">Alexander Hare</a> had similar plans, and hired a captain&#160;&#8211;&#32; coincidentally, Clunies-Ross's brother&#160;&#8211;&#32;to bring him and a volunteer <a href="/wiki/Harem" title="Harem">harem</a> of 40 <a href="/wiki/Malays_(ethnic_group)" title="Malays (ethnic group)">Malay</a> women to the islands, where he hoped to establish his private residence.<sup id="cite_ref-joshua_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-joshua-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> Hare had previously served as resident of <a href="/wiki/Banjarmasin" title="Banjarmasin">Banjarmasin</a>, a town in <a href="/wiki/Borneo" title="Borneo">Borneo</a>, and found that "he could not confine himself to the tame life that civilisation affords".<sup id="cite_ref-joshua_22-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-joshua-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Clunies-Ross returned two years later with his wife, children and mother-in-law, and found Hare already established on the island and living with the private harem. A feud grew between the two.<sup id="cite_ref-joshua_22-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-joshua-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> Clunies-Ross's eight sailors "began at once the invasion of the new kingdom to take possession of it, women and all".<sup id="cite_ref-joshua_22-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-joshua-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After some time, Hare's women began deserting him, and instead finding themselves partners amongst Clunies-Ross's sailors.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> Disheartened, Hare left the island. He died in <a href="/wiki/Bencoolen_(city)" class="mw-redirect" title="Bencoolen (city)">Bencoolen</a> in 1834.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> Encouraged by members of the former harem, Clunies-Ross then recruited Malays to come to the island for work and wives.
</p><p>Clunies-Ross's workers were paid in a currency called the Cocos rupee, a currency John Clunies-Ross minted himself that could only be redeemed at the company store.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Chart_of_Cocos_Keeling_Islands.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/Chart_of_Cocos_Keeling_Islands.png/220px-Chart_of_Cocos_Keeling_Islands.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="297" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/Chart_of_Cocos_Keeling_Islands.png/330px-Chart_of_Cocos_Keeling_Islands.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/Chart_of_Cocos_Keeling_Islands.png/440px-Chart_of_Cocos_Keeling_Islands.png 2x" data-file-width="872" data-file-height="1179" /></a><figcaption>1840 chart of the Cocos (Keeling) Islands</figcaption></figure>
<p>On 1 April 1836, <a href="/wiki/HMS_Beagle" title="HMS Beagle">HMS&#160;<i>Beagle</i></a> under Captain <a href="/wiki/Robert_FitzRoy" title="Robert FitzRoy">Robert FitzRoy</a> arrived to take soundings to establish the profile of the atoll as part of the <a href="/wiki/Second_voyage_of_HMS_Beagle" title="Second voyage of HMS Beagle">survey expedition of the <i>Beagle</i></a>. To the naturalist <a href="/wiki/Charles_Darwin" title="Charles Darwin">Charles Darwin</a>, aboard the ship, the results supported a theory he had developed of how atolls formed, which he later published as <i><a href="/wiki/The_Structure_and_Distribution_of_Coral_Reefs" title="The Structure and Distribution of Coral Reefs">The Structure and Distribution of Coral Reefs</a></i>. He studied the natural history of the islands and collected specimens.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> Darwin's assistant <a href="/wiki/Syms_Covington" title="Syms Covington">Syms Covington</a> noted that "an Englishman [he was in fact Scottish] and HIS family, with about sixty or seventy <a href="/wiki/Mulatto" title="Mulatto">mulattos</a> from the <a href="/wiki/Cape_of_Good_Hope" title="Cape of Good Hope">Cape of Good Hope</a>, live on one of the islands. Captain Ross, the governor, is now absent at the Cape."
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Annexation_by_the_British_Empire">Annexation by the British Empire</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Annexation by the British Empire">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The islands were annexed by the British Empire in 1857.<sup id="cite_ref-Kenneth_Roberts-Wray_1966._p._882_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kenneth_Roberts-Wray_1966._p._882-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> This annexation was carried out by Captain <a href="/wiki/Stephen_Grenville_Fremantle" title="Stephen Grenville Fremantle">Stephen Grenville Fremantle</a> in command of <a href="/wiki/HMS_Juno_(1844)" title="HMS Juno (1844)">HMS&#160;<i>Juno</i></a>. Fremantle claimed the islands for the British Empire and appointed <a href="/wiki/King_of_the_Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="King of the Cocos (Keeling) Islands">Ross II</a> as Superintendent.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> In 1878, by <a href="/wiki/Letters_Patent" class="mw-redirect" title="Letters Patent">Letters Patent</a>, the Governor of <a href="/wiki/Ceylon" class="mw-redirect" title="Ceylon">Ceylon</a> was made Governor of the islands, and, by further Letters Patent in 1886,<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> responsibility for the islands was transferred to the Governor of the <a href="/wiki/Straits_Settlement" class="mw-redirect" title="Straits Settlement">Straits Settlement</a> to exercise his functions as "Governor of Cocos Islands".<sup id="cite_ref-Kenneth_Roberts-Wray_1966._p._882_27-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kenneth_Roberts-Wray_1966._p._882-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The islands were made part of the Straits Settlement under an Order in Council of 20 May 1903.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> Meanwhile, in 1886 <a href="/wiki/Queen_Victoria" title="Queen Victoria">Queen Victoria</a> had, by indenture, granted the islands in perpetuity to John Clunies-Ross.<sup id="cite_ref-Kenneth_Roberts-Wray_1966._p._883_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kenneth_Roberts-Wray_1966._p._883-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> The head of the family enjoyed semi-official status as <a href="/wiki/Resident_Magistrate" class="mw-redirect" title="Resident Magistrate">Resident Magistrate</a> and Government representative.<sup id="cite_ref-Kenneth_Roberts-Wray_1966._p._883_31-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kenneth_Roberts-Wray_1966._p._883-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1901 a <a href="/wiki/Telegraph" class="mw-redirect" title="Telegraph">telegraph</a> cable station was established on Direction Island. Undersea cables went to <a href="/wiki/Rodrigues" title="Rodrigues">Rodrigues</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mauritius" title="Mauritius">Mauritius</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jakarta" title="Jakarta">Batavia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Java" title="Java">Java</a> and <a href="/wiki/Fremantle" title="Fremantle">Fremantle</a>, <a href="/wiki/Western_Australia" title="Western Australia">Western Australia</a>. In 1910 a wireless station was established to communicate with passing ships. The cable station ceased operation in 1966.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="World_War_I">World War I</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: World War I">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:WW1_Landing_at_Direction_Island.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/WW1_Landing_at_Direction_Island.jpg/220px-WW1_Landing_at_Direction_Island.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/WW1_Landing_at_Direction_Island.jpg/330px-WW1_Landing_at_Direction_Island.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/WW1_Landing_at_Direction_Island.jpg/440px-WW1_Landing_at_Direction_Island.jpg 2x" data-file-width="450" data-file-height="338" /></a><figcaption>A landing party from the <a href="/wiki/Imperial_German_Navy" title="Imperial German Navy">German Navy</a> cruiser <i><a href="/wiki/SMS_Emden_(1908)" class="mw-redirect" title="SMS Emden (1908)">Emden</a></i> leaves the Cocos (Keeling) Islands via this jetty on <a href="/wiki/Direction_Island,_Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands" title="Direction Island, Cocos (Keeling) Islands">Direction Island</a> on 9 November 1914.</figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Cocos" title="Battle of Cocos">Battle of Cocos</a></div>
<p>On the morning of 9 November 1914, the islands became the site of the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Cocos" title="Battle of Cocos">Battle of Cocos</a>, one of the first naval battles of <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a>. A landing party from the German cruiser <a href="/wiki/SMS_Emden_(1906)" class="mw-redirect" title="SMS Emden (1906)">SMS&#160;<i>Emden</i></a> captured and disabled the wireless and cable communications station on <a href="/wiki/Direction_Island,_Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands" title="Direction Island, Cocos (Keeling) Islands">Direction Island</a>, but not before the station was able to transmit a distress call. An Allied troop convoy was passing nearby, and the Australian cruiser <a href="/wiki/HMAS_Sydney_(1912)" title="HMAS Sydney (1912)">HMAS&#160;<i>Sydney</i></a> was detached from the convoy escort to investigate.
</p><p><i>Sydney</i> spotted the island and <i>Emden</i> at 09:15, with both ships preparing for combat. At 11:20, the heavily damaged <i>Emden</i> beached herself on <a href="/wiki/North_Keeling_Island" class="mw-redirect" title="North Keeling Island">North Keeling Island</a>. The Australian warship broke to pursue <i>Emden</i><span class="nowrap" style="padding-left:0.1em;">&#39;</span>s supporting <a href="/wiki/Collier_(ship)" title="Collier (ship)">collier</a>, which scuttled herself, then returned to North Keeling Island at 16:00. At this point, <i>Emden</i><span class="nowrap" style="padding-left:0.1em;">&#39;</span>s <a href="/wiki/Battle_ensign" title="Battle ensign">battle ensign</a> was still flying: usually a sign that a ship intends to continue fighting. After no response to instructions to lower the ensign, two salvoes were shot into the beached cruiser, after which the Germans lowered the flag and raised a <a href="/wiki/Flag_of_surrender" class="mw-redirect" title="Flag of surrender">white sheet</a>. <i>Sydney</i> had orders to ascertain the status of the transmission station, but returned the next day to provide medical assistance to the Germans.
</p><p>Casualties totaled 134 personnel aboard <i>Emden</i> killed, and 69 wounded, compared to four killed and 16 wounded aboard <i>Sydney</i>. The German survivors were taken aboard the Australian cruiser, which caught up to the troop convoy in <a href="/wiki/Colombo" title="Colombo">Colombo</a> on 15 November, then transported to <a href="/wiki/Malta" title="Malta">Malta</a> and handed over the prisoners to the <a href="/wiki/British_Army" title="British Army">British Army</a>. An additional 50 German personnel from the shore party, unable to be recovered before <i>Sydney</i> arrived, commandeered a schooner and escaped from Direction Island, eventually arriving in <a href="/wiki/Constantinople" title="Constantinople">Constantinople</a>. <i>Emden</i> was the last active <a href="/wiki/Central_Powers" title="Central Powers">Central Powers</a> warship in the Indian or Pacific Ocean, which meant troopships from Australia and New Zealand could sail without naval escort, and Allied ships could be deployed elsewhere.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="World_War_II">World War II</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: World War II">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>During <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, the cable station was once again a vital link. The Cocos were valuable for direction finding by the <a href="/wiki/Y_service" title="Y service">Y service</a>, the worldwide intelligence system used during the war.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Allied planners noted that the islands might be seized as an airfield for <a href="/wiki/Nazi_Germany" title="Nazi Germany">German</a> planes and as a base for commerce raiders operating in the Indian Ocean. Following <a href="/wiki/Empire_of_Japan" title="Empire of Japan">Japan</a>'s entry into the war, Japanese forces occupied neighbouring islands. To avoid drawing their attention to the Cocos cable station and its islands' garrison, the <a href="/wiki/Seaplane" title="Seaplane">seaplane</a> anchorage between Direction and <a href="/wiki/Horsburgh_Island" title="Horsburgh Island">Horsburgh</a> islands was not used. Radio transmitters were also kept silent, except in emergencies.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2009)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>After the <a href="/wiki/Fall_of_Singapore" title="Fall of Singapore">Fall of Singapore</a> in 1942, the islands were administered from Ceylon and West and Direction Islands were placed under <a href="/wiki/Allies_of_World_War_II" title="Allies of World War II">Allied</a> military administration. The islands' garrison initially consisted of a platoon from the British Army's <a href="/wiki/King%27s_African_Rifles" title="King&#39;s African Rifles">King's African Rifles</a>, located on Horsburgh Island, with two 6-inch (152.4&#160;mm) guns to cover the anchorage. The local inhabitants all lived on Home Island. Despite the importance of the islands as a communication centre, the Japanese made no attempt either to raid or to occupy them and contented themselves with sending over a reconnaissance aircraft about once a month.
</p><p>On the night of 8–9 May 1942, 15 members of the garrison, from the <a href="/wiki/Ceylon_Defence_Force" title="Ceylon Defence Force">Ceylon Defence Force</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mutiny" title="Mutiny">mutinied</a> under the leadership of <a href="/wiki/Gratien_Fernando" title="Gratien Fernando">Gratien Fernando</a>. The mutineers were said to have been provoked by the attitude of their British officers and were also supposedly inspired by Japanese anti-British propaganda. They attempted to take control of the <a href="/wiki/Artillery" title="Artillery">gun battery</a> on the islands. The <a href="/wiki/Cocos_Islands_Mutiny" class="mw-redirect" title="Cocos Islands Mutiny">Cocos Islands Mutiny</a> was crushed, but the mutineers murdered one non-mutinous soldier and wounded one officer. Seven of the mutineers were sentenced to death at a trial that was later alleged to have been improperly conducted, though the guilt of the accused was admitted. Four of the sentences were commuted, but three men were executed, including Fernando. These were to be the only <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">British Commonwealth</a> soldiers executed for mutiny during the Second World War.<sup id="cite_ref-CIM_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIM-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 25 December 1942, the Japanese submarine <i><a href="/wiki/Japanese_submarine_I-166" title="Japanese submarine I-166">I-166</a></i> bombarded the islands but caused no damage.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Later in the war, two airstrips were built, and three bomber squadrons were moved to the islands to conduct raids against Japanese targets in South East Asia and to provide support during the <a href="/wiki/Operation_Tiderace" title="Operation Tiderace">planned</a> reinvasion of <a href="/wiki/British_Malaya" title="British Malaya">Malaya</a> and reconquest of Singapore. The first aircraft to arrive were <a href="/wiki/Supermarine_Spitfire" title="Supermarine Spitfire">Supermarine Spitfire</a> Mk VIIIs of <a href="/wiki/No._136_Squadron_RAF" title="No. 136 Squadron RAF">No. 136 Squadron RAF</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> They included some <a href="/wiki/B-24" class="mw-redirect" title="B-24">Liberator</a> bombers from <a href="/wiki/No._321_Squadron_RAF" class="mw-redirect" title="No. 321 Squadron RAF">No. 321 (Netherlands) Squadron RAF</a> (members of exiled Dutch forces serving with the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Air_Force" title="Royal Air Force">Royal Air Force</a>), which were also stationed on the islands. When in July 1945 <a href="/wiki/No._99_Squadron_RAF" title="No. 99 Squadron RAF">No. 99</a> and <a href="/wiki/No._356_Squadron_RAF" title="No. 356 Squadron RAF">No. 356</a> RAF squadrons arrived on West Island, they brought with them a daily newspaper called <i>Atoll</i> which contained news of what was happening in the outside world. Run by airmen in their off-duty hours, it achieved fame when dropped by Liberator bombers on POW camps over the heads of the Japanese guards.
</p><p>In 1946, the administration of the islands reverted to <a href="/wiki/Colony_of_Singapore" title="Colony of Singapore">Singapore</a> and it became part of the Colony of Singapore.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Transfer_to_Australia">Transfer to Australia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Transfer to Australia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>On 23 November 1955, the islands were transferred from the United Kingdom to the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Australia" class="mw-redirect" title="Commonwealth of Australia">Commonwealth of Australia</a>. Immediately before the transfer the islands were part of the United Kingdom's Colony of Singapore, in accordance with the Straits Settlements (Repeal) Act, 1946 of the United Kingdom<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> and the British Settlements Acts, 1887 and 1945, as applied by the Act of 1946.<sup id="cite_ref-Kenneth_Roberts-Wray_1966._p._882_27-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kenneth_Roberts-Wray_1966._p._882-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> The legal steps for effecting the transfer were as follows:<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li>The Commonwealth Parliament and the Government requested and consented to the enactment of a United Kingdom Act for the purpose.</li>
<li>The Cocos Islands Act, 1955, authorized Her Majesty, by Order in Council, to direct that the islands should cease to form part of the <a href="/wiki/Colony_of_Singapore" title="Colony of Singapore">Colony of Singapore</a> and be placed under the authority of the Commonwealth.</li>
<li>By the Cocos (Keeling) Islands Act, 1955, the Parliament of the Commonwealth provided for the acceptance of the islands as a territory under the authority of the Commonwealth and for its government.</li>
<li>The Cocos Islands Order in Council, 1955, made under the United Kingdom Act of 1955, provided that upon the appointed day (23 November 1955) the islands should cease to form part of the Colony of Singapore and be placed under the authority of the Commonwealth of Australia.</li></ul>
<p>The reason for this comparatively complex machinery was due to the terms of the Straits Settlement (Repeal) Act, 1946. According to Sir <a href="/wiki/Kenneth_Roberts-Wray" title="Kenneth Roberts-Wray">Kenneth Roberts-Wray</a> "any other procedure would have been of doubtful validity".<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> The separation involved three steps: separation from the Colony of Singapore; transfer by United Kingdom and acceptance by Australia.
</p><p>H.&#160;J. Hull was appointed the first official representative (now administrator) of the new territory. He had been a lieutenant-commander in the Royal Australian Navy and was released for the purpose. Under Commonwealth Cabinet Decision 1573 of 9 September 1958, Hull's appointment was terminated and <a href="/wiki/John_William_Stokes" title="John William Stokes">John William Stokes</a> was appointed on secondment from the Northern Territory police. A media release at the end of October 1958 by the Minister for Territories, Hasluck, commended Hull's three years of service on Cocos.
</p><p>Stokes served in the position from 31 October 1958 to 30 September 1960. His son's boyhood memories and photos of the Islands have been published.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> C.&#160;I. Buffett <a href="/wiki/Order_of_the_British_Empire" title="Order of the British Empire">MBE</a> from <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Island" title="Norfolk Island">Norfolk Island</a> succeeded him and served from 28 July 1960 to 30 June 1966, and later acted as Administrator back on Cocos and on Norfolk Island. In 1974, Ken Mullen wrote a small book<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> about his time with wife and son from 1964 to 1966 working at the Cable Station on Direction Island.
</p><p>In the 1970s, the Australian government's dissatisfaction with the Clunies-Ross feudal style of rule of the island increased. In 1978, Australia forced the family to sell the islands for the sum of <a href="/wiki/Australian_dollar" title="Australian dollar">A$</a>6,250,000, using the threat of compulsory acquisition. By agreement, the family retained ownership of Oceania House, their home on the island. In 1983, the Australian government reneged on this agreement and told <a href="/wiki/Clunies-Ross_Family#John_Cecil_Clunies-Ross" class="mw-redirect" title="Clunies-Ross Family">John Clunies-Ross</a> that he should leave the Cocos. The following year the <a href="/wiki/High_Court_of_Australia" title="High Court of Australia">High Court of Australia</a> ruled that resumption of Oceania House was unlawful, but the Australian government ordered that no government business was to be granted to Clunies-Ross's shipping company, an action that contributed to his bankruptcy.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> John Clunies-Ross later moved to <a href="/wiki/Perth,_Western_Australia" class="mw-redirect" title="Perth, Western Australia">Perth, Western Australia</a>. However, some members of the Clunies-Ross family still live on the Cocos.
</p><p>Extensive preparations were undertaken by the government of Australia to prepare the Cocos Malays to vote in their referendum of self-determination. Discussions began in 1982, with an aim of holding the referendum, under United Nations supervision, in mid-1983. Under guidelines developed by the UN Decolonization Committee, residents were to be offered three choices: full independence, free association, or integration with Australia. The last option was preferred by both the islanders and the Australian government. A change in government in Canberra following the March 1983 Australian elections delayed the vote by one year. While the Home Island Council stated a preference for a traditional communal consensus "vote", the UN insisted on a secret ballot. <a href="/wiki/1984_Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands_status_referendum" title="1984 Cocos (Keeling) Islands status referendum">The referendum</a> was held on 6 April 1984, with all 261 eligible islanders participating, including the Clunies-Ross family: 229 voted for integration, 21 for Free Association, nine for independence, and two failed to indicate a preference.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> In recent years a series of disputes have occurred between the Muslim and the non-Muslim population of the islands.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Indigenous_status">Indigenous status</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Indigenous status">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Descendants of the Cocos Malays brought to the islands from the <a href="/wiki/Malay_Peninsula" title="Malay Peninsula">Malay Peninsula</a>, the <a href="/wiki/List_of_islands_of_Indonesia" title="List of islands of Indonesia">Indonesian archipelago</a>, <a href="/wiki/Southern_Africa" title="Southern Africa">Southern Africa</a> and <a href="/wiki/New_Guinea" title="New Guinea">New Guinea</a> by Hare and by Clunies-Ross as <a href="/wiki/Indentured_servitude" title="Indentured servitude">indentured workers</a>, <a href="/wiki/Slavery" title="Slavery">slaves</a> or <a href="/wiki/Convict" title="Convict">convicts</a> are as of 2019<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup> seeking recognition from the <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Australia" class="mw-redirect" title="Government of Australia">Australian government</a> to be acknowledged as <a href="/wiki/Indigenous_Australians" title="Indigenous Australians">Indigenous Australians</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Government">Government</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Government">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The capital of the Territory of Cocos (Keeling) Islands is West Island while the largest settlement is the village of Bantam, on Home Island.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2010)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Governance of the islands is based on the <i>Cocos (Keeling) Islands Act 1955</i><sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> and depends heavily on the laws of Australia. The islands are administered from <a href="/wiki/Canberra" title="Canberra">Canberra</a> by the Department of Infrastructure, Transport, Regional Development and Communications through a non-resident <a href="/wiki/Administrator_(Australia)" title="Administrator (Australia)">Administrator</a> appointed by the <a href="/wiki/Governor-General_of_Australia" title="Governor-General of Australia">Governor-General</a>. They were previously the responsibility of the <a href="/wiki/Department_of_Transport_and_Regional_Services" title="Department of Transport and Regional Services">Department of Transport and Regional Services</a> (before 2007), the <a href="/wiki/Attorney-General%27s_Department_(Australia)" title="Attorney-General&#39;s Department (Australia)">Attorney-General's Department</a> (2007–2013), <a href="/wiki/Department_of_Infrastructure_and_Regional_Development" title="Department of Infrastructure and Regional Development">Department of Infrastructure and Regional Development</a> (2013–2017) and <a href="/wiki/Department_of_Infrastructure,_Regional_Development_and_Cities" title="Department of Infrastructure, Regional Development and Cities">Department of Infrastructure, Regional Development and Cities</a> (2017–2020).<sup id="cite_ref-DOTARS_49-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DOTARS-49">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-AussieAG_50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AussieAG-50">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The current Administrator<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items" title="Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers"><span title="The time period mentioned near this tag is ambiguous. (March 2022)">when?</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> is <a href="/wiki/Natasha_Griggs" title="Natasha Griggs">Natasha Griggs</a><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items" title="Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers"><span title="Mrs. Griggs is no longer the Administrator acccording to her Wikipedia page. (February 2023)">needs update</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>, who was appointed on 5 October 2017 and is also the Administrator of <a href="/wiki/Christmas_Island" title="Christmas Island">Christmas Island</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (March 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> These two territories comprise the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Indian_Ocean_Territories" title="Australian Indian Ocean Territories">Australian Indian Ocean Territories</a>. The Australian Government provides Commonwealth-level government services through the Christmas Island Administration and the Department of Infrastructure and Regional Development.<sup id="cite_ref-AAO_Sep_2013_51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AAO_Sep_2013-51">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> As per the Federal Government's <i>Territories Law Reform Act 1992</i>, which came into force on 1 July 1992, Western Australian laws are applied to the Cocos Islands, "so far as they are capable of applying in the Territory";<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> non-application or partial application of such laws is at the discretion of the federal government. The Act also gives Western Australian courts judicial power over the islands. The Cocos Islands remain constitutionally distinct from Western Australia, however; the power of the state to legislate for the territory is power-delegated by the federal government. The kind of services typically provided by a state government elsewhere in Australia are provided by departments of the Western Australian Government, and by contractors, with the costs met by the federal government.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (March 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>There also exists a unicameral <a href="/wiki/Shire_of_Cocos" class="mw-redirect" title="Shire of Cocos">Cocos (Keeling) Islands Shire Council</a> with seven seats. A full term lasts four years, though elections are held every two years; approximately half the members retire each two years.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (March 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> As of March 2022<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup> the president of the shire is Aindil Minkom.<sup id="cite_ref-Birch_2022_53-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Birch_2022-53">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> The next local election is scheduled for 21 October 2023 alongside elections on <a href="/wiki/Christmas_Island" title="Christmas Island">Christmas Island</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Federal_politics">Federal politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Federal politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>

</div>
<p>Cocos (Keeling) Islands residents who are Australian citizens also vote in <a href="/wiki/Elections_in_Australia" title="Elections in Australia">federal elections</a>. Cocos (Keeling) Islanders are represented in the House of Representatives by the member for the <a href="/wiki/Division_of_Lingiari" title="Division of Lingiari">Division of Lingiari</a> (in the <a href="/wiki/Northern_Territory" title="Northern Territory">Northern Territory</a>) and in the Senate by Northern Territory senators.<sup id="cite_ref-AEC_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AEC-57">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> At the <a href="/wiki/2016_Australian_federal_election" title="2016 Australian federal election">2016 federal election</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Labor_Party" title="Australian Labor Party">Labor Party</a> received absolute majorities from Cocos electors in both the House of Representatives and the Senate.<sup id="cite_ref-senate_55-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-senate-55">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-house_56-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-house-56">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Defence_and_law_enforcement">Defence and law enforcement</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Defence and law enforcement">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><span class="anchor" id="Defence"></span><span class="anchor" id="RAAF"></span><span class="anchor" id="Military"></span>
</p><p>Defence is the responsibility of the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Defence_Force" title="Australian Defence Force">Australian Defence Force</a>. Until 2023, there were no active military installations or defence personnel on the island; the administrator could request the assistance of the Australian Defence Force if required. 
</p><p>In 2016, the Australian Department of Defence announced that the <a href="/wiki/Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands_Airport" title="Cocos (Keeling) Islands Airport">Cocos (Keeling) Islands Airport</a> (West Island) would be upgraded to support the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Australian_Air_Force" title="Royal Australian Air Force">Royal Australian Air Force</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Boeing_P-8_Poseidon" title="Boeing P-8 Poseidon">P-8 Poseidon</a> maritime patrol aircraft.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> Work was scheduled to begin in early 2023 and be completed by 2026. The airfield will act as a forward operating base for Australian surveillance and electronic warfare aircraft in the region.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Royal_Australian_Navy" title="Royal Australian Navy">Royal Australian Navy</a> and <a href="/wiki/Australian_Border_Force" title="Australian Border Force">Australian Border Force</a> also deploy <a href="/wiki/Cape-class_patrol_boat" title="Cape-class patrol boat">Cape</a> and <a href="/wiki/Armidale-class_patrol_boat" title="Armidale-class patrol boat"><i>Armidale</i>-class</a> patrol boats to conduct surveillance and counter-migrant smuggling patrols in adjacent waters.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> As of 2023, the Navy's <i>Armidale</i>-class boats are in the process of being replaced by larger <a href="/wiki/Arafura-class_offshore_patrol_vessel" title="Arafura-class offshore patrol vessel"><i>Arafura</i>-class offshore patrol vessels</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Civilian law enforcement and community policing is provided by the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Federal_Police" title="Australian Federal Police">Australian Federal Police</a>. The normal deployment to the island is one <a href="/wiki/Sergeant" title="Sergeant">sergeant</a> and one <a href="/wiki/Constable" title="Constable">constable</a>. These are augmented by two locally engaged Special Members who have police powers.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Courts">Courts</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Courts">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_the_Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands" title="Supreme Court of the Cocos (Keeling) Islands">Supreme Court of the Cocos (Keeling) Islands</a></div>
<p>Since 1992, court services have been provided by the <a href="/wiki/Department_of_the_Attorney_General_(Western_Australia)" class="mw-redirect" title="Department of the Attorney General (Western Australia)">Western Australian Department of the Attorney-General</a> under a service delivery arrangement with the Australian Government. Western Australian Court Services provide Magistrates Court, District Court, Supreme Court, Family Court, Children's Court, Coroner's Court and Registry for births, deaths and marriages and change of name services. Magistrates and judges from Western Australia convene a <a href="/wiki/Circuit_court" title="Circuit court">circuit court</a> as required.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Health_care">Health care</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Health care">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Home Island and West Island have medical clinics providing basic health services, but serious medical conditions and injuries cannot be treated on the island and patients are sent to Perth for treatment, a distance of 3,000&#160;km (1,900&#160;mi).
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Cocos_(keeling)_Islands_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f5/Cocos_%28keeling%29_Islands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/290px-Cocos_%28keeling%29_Islands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="290" height="189" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f5/Cocos_%28keeling%29_Islands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/435px-Cocos_%28keeling%29_Islands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f5/Cocos_%28keeling%29_Islands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/580px-Cocos_%28keeling%29_Islands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a><figcaption>Proportions of exports of the Cocos Islands, 2019</figcaption></figure>
<p>The population of the islands is approximately 600. There is a small and growing tourist industry focused on water-based or nature activities. In 2016, a beach on Direction Island was named the best beach in Australia by <a href="/wiki/Brad_Farmer" title="Brad Farmer">Brad Farmer</a>, an Aquatic and Coastal Ambassador for Tourism Australia and co-author of <i>101 Best Beaches 2017</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Small local gardens and fishing contribute to the food supply, but most food and most other necessities must be imported from Australia or elsewhere.
</p><p>The Cocos Islands Cooperative Society Ltd. employs construction workers, <a href="/wiki/Stevedore" class="mw-redirect" title="Stevedore">stevedores</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Lightering" title="Lightering">lighterage</a> worker operations. Tourism employs others. The unemployment rate was 6.7% in 2011.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Plastic_pollution"><span class="anchor" id="plastic"></span>Plastic pollution</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Plastic pollution">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>A 2019 study led by Jennifer Lavers from the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Tasmania" title="University of Tasmania">University of Tasmania</a>'s Institute of Marine and Antarctic Studies published in the journal <i><a href="/wiki/Scientific_Reports" title="Scientific Reports">Scientific Reports</a></i> estimated the volume of <a href="/wiki/Plastic_pollution" title="Plastic pollution">plastic rubbish</a> on the Islands as around 414 million pieces, weighing 238 tonnes, 93% of which lies buried under the sand. It said that previous surveys which only assessed surface garbage probably "drastically underestimated the scale of debris accumulation". The plastic waste found in the study consisted mostly of single-use items such as bottles, <a href="/wiki/Plastic_cutlery" class="mw-redirect" title="Plastic cutlery">plastic cutlery</a>, bags and <a href="/wiki/Drinking_straw" title="Drinking straw">drinking straws</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Strategic_importance">Strategic importance</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cocos_(Keeling)_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Strategic importance">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+style+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+style+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+link+.ambox{margin-top:-1px}html body.mediawiki .mw-parser-output .ambox.mbox-small-left{margin:4px 1em 4px .ambox-delete{border-left:10px solid #b32424}.mw-parser-output .ambox-content{border-left:10px solid #f28500}.mw-parser-output .ambox-style{border-left:10px solid #fc3}.mw-parser-output .ambox-move{border-left:10px solid #9932cc}.mw-parser-output .ambox-protection{border-left:10px solid #a2a9b1}.mw-parser-output .ambox .mbox-text{border:none;padding:0.25em .mbox-text-span{margin-left:23px!important}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .ambox{margin:0 10%}}</style><table class="box-Update plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Update" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><span typeof="mw:File"><span><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Ambox_current_red_Americas.svg/42px-Ambox_current_red_Americas.svg.png" decoding="async" width="42" height="34" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Ambox_current_red_Americas.svg/63px-Ambox_current_red_Americas.svg.png 1.5x,
